    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, hi. Thanks for seeing me. Honestly, it's... it's kind of weird to even be here, because usually I'm pretty on top of things. I'm a sales rep for a manufacturing company, so I'm always on the go, talking to people, keeping a tight schedule. But... honestly, the thing that actually scared me into coming in today is that my eating has just been completely all over the place lately. 

And I don't just mean skipping a lunch because I got busy with a client or something. I mean, some days I will literally forget meals completely. Like, the whole day will go by, the sun goes down, and I'll suddenly realize I haven't had a single bite of food, not even a protein bar from the console of my car. I just don't even think about it, which is crazy for me. 

But then, it swings the complete opposite way. There are these other nights where I am just tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight. It's like a switch flips and I can't stop. I'll order a ridiculous amount of food on my phone, or I'll just be standing in my kitchen at 2 AM ripping open bags of chips or whatever I can find, eating like I haven't seen food in a month. It's just so extreme, going from absolutely nothing to just gorging myself in the middle of the night, and it really started freaking me out because I've never had this happen before. Usually I'm just a normal three-meals-a-day kind of guy, you know? So seeing myself act like this... yeah, it scared me into making the appointment.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control, really. It's not like I'm sitting there thinking, "Oh God, I have to stop, I have to stop," and my hands are just moving on their own, you know? It's more like... I'm just incredibly hungry all of a sudden. Like, ravenous. And because I probably haven't eaten all day—like I was saying about just totally forgetting to eat when I'm on the road or making calls—by the time 2 AM rolls around, my body is just screaming for calories. 

So when I'm standing there in the kitchen, ripping into a bag of chips or waiting for the delivery guy, I'm fully aware of what I'm doing. I want to eat all of it. I'm choosing to eat it because it tastes so good in that moment and I feel like I need it to make up for the whole day. It's just the amount that's weird for me. I'll order, like, two large pizzas and some wings, and I'll actually eat most of it, which is insane for one guy. But I never feel like some outside force is making me do it or that I couldn't put the slice down if I really wanted to. I just... don't want to put it down. I just want to keep eating until I'm full, which just takes a lot more food than it ever used to. It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. 

But as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I don't even really take aspirin unless I have a splitting headache, so I'm not one to mess around with pills or anything like that. 

And the exercise thing... well, I used to go to the gym pretty regularly, back when I was on a normal schedule. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.

It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that these episodes of eating a lot happen regularly, maybe once a week or more, and do you ever try to make up for them by doing something like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing a lot of extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. \n\nBut as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I don't even really take aspirin unless I have a splitting headache, so I'm not one to mess around with pills or anything like that. \n\nAnd the exercise thing... well, I used to go to the gym pretty regularly, back when I was on a normal schedule. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo these episodes of eating a lot or skipping meals cause you a lot of distress, or do they interfere with your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"My manager has started pulling me off accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t, and HR got involved after the employee-assistance call. I’m still showing up, but work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, distress is... well, yes. It’s definitely causing problems. Because, like I was saying earlier, I’m usually so on top of things, right? In wholesale, you have to be. You’re juggling a dozen clients, tracking supply chains, and if you drop the ball, it’s a disaster. But lately, with my schedule being so messed up and the eating thing, the crashing and the late-night stuff... I’m just not sharp. And it’s really starting to bleed into my job in a way that I just can't hide anymore. 

My manager actually pulled me aside recently, which was incredibly humiliating, to be honest. He’s started pulling me off some of my bigger accounts. These are accounts I built from the ground up! But... I guess I can't totally blame him, because I’ve been making these stupid mistakes that I normally would never, ever make. Like, transposing numbers on a massive manufacturing order or completely blanking on a follow-up call. It got so bad and I was so stressed out about it that I actually called the employee-assistance line the company has. I just didn't know who else to talk to or what to do. 

But then HR got involved after that employee-assistance call, which is just a whole other nightmare because now I feel like I’m under a microscope every time I walk into the office. They're sending me emails, checking in, and it just makes me feel worse. I mean, I’m still showing up every day. I’m putting on the suit, getting in the car, doing the drive. I'm physically present. But honestly? Between the job going sideways and my apartment just being littered with late-night pizza boxes and feeling like a mess... work and home both just feel like they’re completely slipping out of my control right now. And I hate that feeling. I've always been the guy who has it together, so feeling like I'm losing my grip on everything is... yeah, it's really distressing.

I just want to keep eating until I'm full, which just takes a lot more food than it ever used to. It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that these episodes of eating a lot happen regularly, maybe once a week or more, and do you ever try to make up for them by doing something like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing a lot of extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. \n\nBut as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I don't even really take aspirin unless I have a splitting headache, so I'm not one to mess around with pills or anything like that. \n\nAnd the exercise thing... well, I used to go to the gym pretty regularly, back when I was on a normal schedule. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot or skipping meals cause you a lot of distress, or do they interfere with your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, distress is... well, yes. It’s definitely causing problems. Because, like I was saying earlier, I’m usually so on top of things, right? In wholesale, you have to be. You’re juggling a dozen clients, tracking supply chains, and if you drop the ball, it’s a disaster. But lately, with my schedule being so messed up and the eating thing, the crashing and the late-night stuff... I’m just not sharp. And it’s really starting to bleed into my job in a way that I just can't hide anymore. \n\nMy manager actually pulled me aside recently, which was incredibly humiliating, to be honest. He’s started pulling me off some of my bigger accounts. These are accounts I built from the ground up! But... I guess I can't totally blame him, because I’ve been making these stupid mistakes that I normally would never, ever make. Like, transposing numbers on a massive manufacturing order or completely blanking on a follow-up call. It got so bad and I was so stressed out about it that I actually called the employee-assistance line the company has. I just didn't know who else to talk to or what to do. \n\nBut then HR got involved after that employee-assistance call, which is just a whole other nightmare because now I feel like I’m under a microscope every time I walk into the office. They're sending me emails, checking in, and it just makes me feel worse. I mean, I’m still showing up every day. I’m putting on the suit, getting in the car, doing the drive. I'm physically present. But honestly? Between the job going sideways and my apartment just being littered with late-night pizza boxes and feeling like a mess... work and home both just feel like they’re completely slipping out of my control right now. And I hate that feeling. I've always been the guy who has it together, so feeling like I'm losing my grip on everything is... yeah, it's really distressing.\n\n[Last Question]\nOver the past two weeks, have you experienced days where you feel down or depressed most of the day, or have lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Down or depressed... yeah, I guess that’s a good way to put it. But it’s almost more like... it’s like everything has just gone gray. The things I used to actually look forward to just feel like chores now, or I just avoid them entirely. 

Like, take my weekends, for example. I’ve been in this Sunday league for soccer for, man, probably three years now. We’re not professionals or anything, obviously, but it was a big deal to me. I was always the guy organizing the post-game drinks, making sure everyone showed up, getting the team hyped. But the last couple of weeks? I haven’t even replied to the group chat. I just... I look at my phone, I see the messages rolling in about who's bringing the net or whatever, and I can’t even muster the energy to type "I’m out." I just leave them on read. I don't care at all. 

And it’s not just soccer, it’s literally everything. My girlfriend, she’s been trying to be patient with me, but I used to really love cooking for her. I’d actually go out of my way to hit the good grocery store, pick out fresh ingredients, do this whole elaborate pasta from scratch or a really nice steak dinner. It was our thing, you know? A way to unwind and connect after a crazy week of driving and sales calls. Now, the thought of standing in the kitchen and chopping an onion feels like I'm being asked to climb Mount Everest. We’ve just been getting takeout, or like I said, I don't eat at all until those 2 AM binges. I just can’t make myself care about making a nice meal anymore. 

We used to do this other thing, too, where we’d go check out these small art galleries downtown on Saturday afternoons. I’m not some huge art expert or anything, but I always liked the vibe, seeing what local people were creating, just walking around and talking about it. Now, she asks if I want to go and I just stare at the wall. The whole idea just seems so incredibly pointless to me right now. 

Even at work—and I know I told you work is going terribly right now—but even the parts I used to actually like are just dead to me. I always took pride in helping the newer sales guys. You know, the young guys fresh out of school who don’t really know how to talk to a tough manufacturing client yet. I used to sit with them, review their pitches, give them pointers on how to close. It felt good to be the guy they looked up to. But last week, this new guy came up to my desk asking for advice on a tricky account, and I just completely brushed him off. I didn’t even look at his file. I just told him he needed to figure it out himself and put my headphones on. I just... I can't make myself care about any of it. Not the soccer, not the cooking, the galleries, the mentoring... it’s like someone just unplugged me from everything that used to make me feel like myself. So yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down.

Usually I'm just a normal three-meals-a-day kind of guy, you know? So seeing myself act like this... yeah, it scared me into making the appointment.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating a lot of food in a short period of time, like in the middle of the night, do you feel like you can’t stop or control how much you eat during those moments?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control, really. It's not like I'm sitting there thinking, \"Oh God, I have to stop, I have to stop,\" and my hands are just moving on their own, you know? It's more like... I'm just incredibly hungry all of a sudden. Like, ravenous. And because I probably haven't eaten all day—like I was saying about just totally forgetting to eat when I'm on the road or making calls—by the time 2 AM rolls around, my body is just screaming for calories. \n\nSo when I'm standing there in the kitchen, ripping into a bag of chips or waiting for the delivery guy, I'm fully aware of what I'm doing. I want to eat all of it. I'm choosing to eat it because it tastes so good in that moment and I feel like I need it to make up for the whole day. It's just the amount that's weird for me. I'll order, like, two large pizzas and some wings, and I'll actually eat most of it, which is insane for one guy. But I never feel like some outside force is making me do it or that I couldn't put the slice down if I really wanted to. I just... don't want to put it down. I just want to keep eating until I'm full, which just takes a lot more food than it ever used to. It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that these episodes of eating a lot happen regularly, maybe once a week or more, and do you ever try to make up for them by doing something like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing a lot of extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. \n\nBut as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I don't even really take aspirin unless I have a splitting headache, so I'm not one to mess around with pills or anything like that. \n\nAnd the exercise thing... well, I used to go to the gym pretty regularly, back when I was on a normal schedule. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot or skipping meals cause you a lot of distress, or do they interfere with your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, distress is... well, yes. It’s definitely causing problems. Because, like I was saying earlier, I’m usually so on top of things, right? In wholesale, you have to be. You’re juggling a dozen clients, tracking supply chains, and if you drop the ball, it’s a disaster. But lately, with my schedule being so messed up and the eating thing, the crashing and the late-night stuff... I’m just not sharp. And it’s really starting to bleed into my job in a way that I just can't hide anymore. \n\nMy manager actually pulled me aside recently, which was incredibly humiliating, to be honest. He’s started pulling me off some of my bigger accounts. These are accounts I built from the ground up! But... I guess I can't totally blame him, because I’ve been making these stupid mistakes that I normally would never, ever make. Like, transposing numbers on a massive manufacturing order or completely blanking on a follow-up call. It got so bad and I was so stressed out about it that I actually called the employee-assistance line the company has. I just didn't know who else to talk to or what to do. \n\nBut then HR got involved after that employee-assistance call, which is just a whole other nightmare because now I feel like I’m under a microscope every time I walk into the office. They're sending me emails, checking in, and it just makes me feel worse. I mean, I’m still showing up every day. I’m putting on the suit, getting in the car, doing the drive. I'm physically present. But honestly? Between the job going sideways and my apartment just being littered with late-night pizza boxes and feeling like a mess... work and home both just feel like they’re completely slipping out of my control right now. And I hate that feeling. I've always been the guy who has it together, so feeling like I'm losing my grip on everything is... yeah, it's really distressing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you experienced days where you feel down or depressed most of the day, or have lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Down or depressed... yeah, I guess that’s a good way to put it. But it’s almost more like... it’s like everything has just gone gray. The things I used to actually look forward to just feel like chores now, or I just avoid them entirely. \n\nLike, take my weekends, for example. I’ve been in this Sunday league for soccer for, man, probably three years now. We’re not professionals or anything, obviously, but it was a big deal to me. I was always the guy organizing the post-game drinks, making sure everyone showed up, getting the team hyped. But the last couple of weeks? I haven’t even replied to the group chat. I just... I look at my phone, I see the messages rolling in about who's bringing the net or whatever, and I can’t even muster the energy to type \"I’m out.\" I just leave them on read. I don't care at all. \n\nAnd it’s not just soccer, it’s literally everything. My girlfriend, she’s been trying to be patient with me, but I used to really love cooking for her. I’d actually go out of my way to hit the good grocery store, pick out fresh ingredients, do this whole elaborate pasta from scratch or a really nice steak dinner. It was our thing, you know? A way to unwind and connect after a crazy week of driving and sales calls. Now, the thought of standing in the kitchen and chopping an onion feels like I'm being asked to climb Mount Everest. We’ve just been getting takeout, or like I said, I don't eat at all until those 2 AM binges. I just can’t make myself care about making a nice meal anymore. \n\nWe used to do this other thing, too, where we’d go check out these small art galleries downtown on Saturday afternoons. I’m not some huge art expert or anything, but I always liked the vibe, seeing what local people were creating, just walking around and talking about it. Now, she asks if I want to go and I just stare at the wall. The whole idea just seems so incredibly pointless to me right now. \n\nEven at work—and I know I told you work is going terribly right now—but even the parts I used to actually like are just dead to me. I always took pride in helping the newer sales guys. You know, the young guys fresh out of school who don’t really know how to talk to a tough manufacturing client yet. I used to sit with them, review their pitches, give them pointers on how to close. It felt good to be the guy they looked up to. But last week, this new guy came up to my desk asking for advice on a tricky account, and I just completely brushed him off. I didn’t even look at his file. I just told him he needed to figure it out himself and put my headphones on. I just... I can't make myself care about any of it. Not the soccer, not the cooking, the galleries, the mentoring... it’s like someone just unplugged me from everything that used to make me feel like myself. So yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Not the soccer, not the cooking, the galleries, the mentoring... it’s like someone just unplugged me from everything that used to make me feel like myself. So yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place, like some days I forget meals completely and other nights I'm tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly, like I was kind of touching on before... that right there is the main thing. The eating stuff is actually the exact thing that scared me into coming in to see you in the first place. Because my appetite has just been completely all over the place, and I mean, it makes zero sense to me anymore. It’s like my body doesn't know what it wants. 

Some days, I will literally just forget meals completely. I’ll be driving around to the warehouses, dealing with the suppliers, and I’ll get to, like, seven o'clock at night and realize I haven't eaten a single solid thing all day. Not even a passing thought about food. My stomach doesn't even growl, it's like the hunger signal is just broken. But then... then you have these other nights where it’s the total extreme opposite. I'll be up past midnight, and suddenly it's like this switch flips and I am just absolutely ravenous. I end up tearing through whatever takeout I can get delivered that late, plus whatever random snacks are sitting around in my cupboards. I'm talking, like, half a pizza, a family-sized bag of chips, cookies, just tearing through wrappers and eating whatever I can get my hands on until I feel completely gross. 

And because of this whole chaotic cycle—the fasting all day and then the massive midnight binges—my weight is just... I don't even know, to be honest. I refuse to step on a scale right now. Some days my suit pants feel loose because I haven't eaten in two days, and other days the waistband is digging into me from all the late-night junk food and bloating. It's just a constant back-and-forth, and realizing how out of control that specific part of my routine has gotten... yeah, that's really what terrified me enough to finally pick up the phone and make this appointment. I just can't keep living like a pendulum swinging between starving and stuffed.

I'm a sales rep for a manufacturing company, so I'm always on the go, talking to people, keeping a tight schedule. But... honestly, the thing that actually scared me into coming in today is that my eating has just been completely all over the place lately. \n\nAnd I don't just mean skipping a lunch because I got busy with a client or something. I mean, some days I will literally forget meals completely. Like, the whole day will go by, the sun goes down, and I'll suddenly realize I haven't had a single bite of food, not even a protein bar from the console of my car. There are these other nights where I am just tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight. It's like a switch flips and I can't stop. I'll order a ridiculous amount of food on my phone, or I'll just be standing in my kitchen at 2 AM ripping open bags of chips or whatever I can find, eating like I haven't seen food in a month. It's just so extreme, going from absolutely nothing to just gorging myself in the middle of the night, and it really started freaking me out because I've never had this happen before. Usually I'm just a normal three-meals-a-day kind of guy, you know? So seeing myself act like this... yeah, it scared me into making the appointment.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating a lot of food in a short period of time, like in the middle of the night, do you feel like you can’t stop or control how much you eat during those moments?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control, really. It's not like I'm sitting there thinking, \"Oh God, I have to stop, I have to stop,\" and my hands are just moving on their own, you know? It's more like... I'm just incredibly hungry all of a sudden. Like, ravenous. And because I probably haven't eaten all day—like I was saying about just totally forgetting to eat when I'm on the road or making calls—by the time 2 AM rolls around, my body is just screaming for calories. \n\nSo when I'm standing there in the kitchen, ripping into a bag of chips or waiting for the delivery guy, I'm fully aware of what I'm doing. I want to eat all of it. I'm choosing to eat it because it tastes so good in that moment and I feel like I need it to make up for the whole day. It's just the amount that's weird for me. I'll order, like, two large pizzas and some wings, and I'll actually eat most of it, which is insane for one guy. But I never feel like some outside force is making me do it or that I couldn't put the slice down if I really wanted to. I just... don't want to put it down. I just want to keep eating until I'm full, which just takes a lot more food than it ever used to. It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that these episodes of eating a lot happen regularly, maybe once a week or more, and do you ever try to make up for them by doing something like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing a lot of extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. \n\nBut as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I don't even really take aspirin unless I have a splitting headache, so I'm not one to mess around with pills or anything like that. \n\nAnd the exercise thing... well, I used to go to the gym pretty regularly, back when I was on a normal schedule. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot or skipping meals cause you a lot of distress, or do they interfere with your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, distress is... well, yes. It’s definitely causing problems. Because, like I was saying earlier, I’m usually so on top of things, right? In wholesale, you have to be. You’re juggling a dozen clients, tracking supply chains, and if you drop the ball, it’s a disaster. But lately, with my schedule being so messed up and the eating thing, the crashing and the late-night stuff... I’m just not sharp. And it’s really starting to bleed into my job in a way that I just can't hide anymore. \n\nMy manager actually pulled me aside recently, which was incredibly humiliating, to be honest. He’s started pulling me off some of my bigger accounts. These are accounts I built from the ground up! But... I guess I can't totally blame him, because I’ve been making these stupid mistakes that I normally would never, ever make. Like, transposing numbers on a massive manufacturing order or completely blanking on a follow-up call. It got so bad and I was so stressed out about it that I actually called the employee-assistance line the company has. I just didn't know who else to talk to or what to do. \n\nBut then HR got involved after that employee-assistance call, which is just a whole other nightmare because now I feel like I’m under a microscope every time I walk into the office. They're sending me emails, checking in, and it just makes me feel worse. I mean, I’m still showing up every day. I’m putting on the suit, getting in the car, doing the drive. I'm physically present. But honestly? Between the job going sideways and my apartment just being littered with late-night pizza boxes and feeling like a mess... work and home both just feel like they’re completely slipping out of my control right now. And I hate that feeling. I've always been the guy who has it together, so feeling like I'm losing my grip on everything is... yeah, it's really distressing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you experienced days where you feel down or depressed most of the day, or have lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Down or depressed... yeah, I guess that’s a good way to put it. But it’s almost more like... it’s like everything has just gone gray. The things I used to actually look forward to just feel like chores now, or I just avoid them entirely. \n\nLike, take my weekends, for example. I’ve been in this Sunday league for soccer for, man, probably three years now. We’re not professionals or anything, obviously, but it was a big deal to me. I was always the guy organizing the post-game drinks, making sure everyone showed up, getting the team hyped. But the last couple of weeks? I haven’t even replied to the group chat. I just... I look at my phone, I see the messages rolling in about who's bringing the net or whatever, and I can’t even muster the energy to type \"I’m out.\" I just leave them on read. I don't care at all. \n\nAnd it’s not just soccer, it’s literally everything. My girlfriend, she’s been trying to be patient with me, but I used to really love cooking for her. I’d actually go out of my way to hit the good grocery store, pick out fresh ingredients, do this whole elaborate pasta from scratch or a really nice steak dinner. It was our thing, you know? A way to unwind and connect after a crazy week of driving and sales calls. Now, the thought of standing in the kitchen and chopping an onion feels like I'm being asked to climb Mount Everest. We’ve just been getting takeout, or like I said, I don't eat at all until those 2 AM binges. I just can’t make myself care about making a nice meal anymore. \n\nWe used to do this other thing, too, where we’d go check out these small art galleries downtown on Saturday afternoons. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, like I was saying earlier about work, it’s... it’s really bad. The concentration thing is huge right now. Because, you know, in sales, you have to be quick. You have to be able to read a room, remember a client's last order, know the profit margins off the top of your head. But lately? It’s like my brain is just wading through wet cement. 

I’ll be sitting at my desk, right? And I’ll have my CRM software open—that’s the program we use to track all the client interactions and orders and stuff—and I’ll just stare at it. I'll click on a client's profile, maybe it's this big account I have out in Ohio, and I know I need to make a call to negotiate a new bulk rate for these industrial fasteners we sell. Normally, I'd look at their history, instantly know we can offer a five percent discount, pick up the phone, and close it in ten minutes. But now... I just look at the screen. The cursor is just blinking and blinking, and I can't even process what I'm looking at. I'll read the same email from my supplier like four or five times, and by the end of it, I still have absolutely no idea what they just said. It’s like the words are just floating there, completely disconnected from any meaning. 

And making decisions? Forget about it. It’s not just the big work decisions, either. It’s the stupid, everyday stuff. Like, I’ll get in my car in the morning to start my route, and I’ll just sit in my driveway with the engine running for, I don't know, twenty minutes, just agonizing over whether to take the interstate or the local roads. I'll think about the construction on I-95, and then I'll think about the school zones on the back roads, and I just... I can't pick. I literally cannot make my brain choose a path. It’s paralyzing. 

That’s exactly how I ended up transposing those numbers on that massive order I told you about earlier. I was looking right at the invoice, I knew what the quantity was supposed to be, but my focus was just so completely shot that my hands typed something entirely different and my brain didn't even catch it. It's exhausting, honestly. You spend your whole day just trying to force your brain to work the way it used to, and it just refuses to cooperate.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n7\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF33.9, F43.20\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, hi. Thanks for seeing me. Honestly, it's... it's kind of weird to even be here, because usually I'm pretty on top of things. I'm a sales rep for a manufacturing company, so I'm always on the go, talking to people, keeping a tight schedule. But... honestly, the thing that actually scared me into coming in today is that my eating has just been completely all over the place lately. \n\nAnd I don't just mean skipping a lunch because I got busy with a client or something. I mean, some days I will literally forget meals completely. Like, the whole day will go by, the sun goes down, and I'll suddenly realize I haven't had a single bite of food, not even a protein bar from the console of my car. I just don't even think about it, which is crazy for me. \n\nBut then, it swings the complete opposite way. There are these other nights where I am just tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight. It's like a switch flips and I can't stop. I'll order a ridiculous amount of food on my phone, or I'll just be standing in my kitchen at 2 AM ripping open bags of chips or whatever I can find, eating like I haven't seen food in a month. It's just so extreme, going from absolutely nothing to just gorging myself in the middle of the night, and it really started freaking me out because I've never had this happen before. Usually I'm just a normal three-meals-a-day kind of guy, you know? So seeing myself act like this... yeah, it scared me into making the appointment.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating a lot of food in a short period of time, like in the middle of the night, do you feel like you can’t stop or control how much you eat during those moments?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control, really. It's not like I'm sitting there thinking, \"Oh God, I have to stop, I have to stop,\" and my hands are just moving on their own, you know? It's more like... I'm just incredibly hungry all of a sudden. Like, ravenous. And because I probably haven't eaten all day—like I was saying about just totally forgetting to eat when I'm on the road or making calls—by the time 2 AM rolls around, my body is just screaming for calories. \n\nSo when I'm standing there in the kitchen, ripping into a bag of chips or waiting for the delivery guy, I'm fully aware of what I'm doing. I want to eat all of it. I'm choosing to eat it because it tastes so good in that moment and I feel like I need it to make up for the whole day. It's just the amount that's weird for me. I'll order, like, two large pizzas and some wings, and I'll actually eat most of it, which is insane for one guy. But I never feel like some outside force is making me do it or that I couldn't put the slice down if I really wanted to. I just... don't want to put it down. I just want to keep eating until I'm full, which just takes a lot more food than it ever used to. It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that these episodes of eating a lot happen regularly, maybe once a week or more, and do you ever try to make up for them by doing something like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing a lot of extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. \n\nBut as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I don't even really take aspirin unless I have a splitting headache, so I'm not one to mess around with pills or anything like that. \n\nAnd the exercise thing... well, I used to go to the gym pretty regularly, back when I was on a normal schedule. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot or skipping meals cause you a lot of distress, or do they interfere with your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, distress is... well, yes. It’s definitely causing problems. Because, like I was saying earlier, I’m usually so on top of things, right? In wholesale, you have to be. You’re juggling a dozen clients, tracking supply chains, and if you drop the ball, it’s a disaster. But lately, with my schedule being so messed up and the eating thing, the crashing and the late-night stuff... I’m just not sharp. And it’s really starting to bleed into my job in a way that I just can't hide anymore. \n\nMy manager actually pulled me aside recently, which was incredibly humiliating, to be honest. He’s started pulling me off some of my bigger accounts. These are accounts I built from the ground up! But... I guess I can't totally blame him, because I’ve been making these stupid mistakes that I normally would never, ever make. Like, transposing numbers on a massive manufacturing order or completely blanking on a follow-up call. It got so bad and I was so stressed out about it that I actually called the employee-assistance line the company has. I just didn't know who else to talk to or what to do. \n\nBut then HR got involved after that employee-assistance call, which is just a whole other nightmare because now I feel like I’m under a microscope every time I walk into the office. They're sending me emails, checking in, and it just makes me feel worse. I mean, I’m still showing up every day. I’m putting on the suit, getting in the car, doing the drive. I'm physically present. But honestly? Between the job going sideways and my apartment just being littered with late-night pizza boxes and feeling like a mess... work and home both just feel like they’re completely slipping out of my control right now. And I hate that feeling. I've always been the guy who has it together, so feeling like I'm losing my grip on everything is... yeah, it's really distressing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you experienced days where you feel down or depressed most of the day, or have lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Down or depressed... yeah, I guess that’s a good way to put it. But it’s almost more like... it’s like everything has just gone gray. The things I used to actually look forward to just feel like chores now, or I just avoid them entirely. \n\nLike, take my weekends, for example. I’ve been in this Sunday league for soccer for, man, probably three years now. We’re not professionals or anything, obviously, but it was a big deal to me. I was always the guy organizing the post-game drinks, making sure everyone showed up, getting the team hyped. But the last couple of weeks? I haven’t even replied to the group chat. I just... I look at my phone, I see the messages rolling in about who's bringing the net or whatever, and I can’t even muster the energy to type \"I’m out.\" I just leave them on read. I don't care at all. \n\nAnd it’s not just soccer, it’s literally everything. My girlfriend, she’s been trying to be patient with me, but I used to really love cooking for her. I’d actually go out of my way to hit the good grocery store, pick out fresh ingredients, do this whole elaborate pasta from scratch or a really nice steak dinner. It was our thing, you know? A way to unwind and connect after a crazy week of driving and sales calls. Now, the thought of standing in the kitchen and chopping an onion feels like I'm being asked to climb Mount Everest. We’ve just been getting takeout, or like I said, I don't eat at all until those 2 AM binges. I just can’t make myself care about making a nice meal anymore. \n\nWe used to do this other thing, too, where we’d go check out these small art galleries downtown on Saturday afternoons. I’m not some huge art expert or anything, but I always liked the vibe, seeing what local people were creating, just walking around and talking about it. Now, she asks if I want to go and I just stare at the wall. The whole idea just seems so incredibly pointless to me right now. \n\nEven at work—and I know I told you work is going terribly right now—but even the parts I used to actually like are just dead to me. I always took pride in helping the newer sales guys. You know, the young guys fresh out of school who don’t really know how to talk to a tough manufacturing client yet. I used to sit with them, review their pitches, give them pointers on how to close. It felt good to be the guy they looked up to. But last week, this new guy came up to my desk asking for advice on a tricky account, and I just completely brushed him off. I didn’t even look at his file. I just told him he needed to figure it out himself and put my headphones on. I just... I can't make myself care about any of it. Not the soccer, not the cooking, the galleries, the mentoring... it’s like someone just unplugged me from everything that used to make me feel like myself. So yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\nPatient: Honestly, like I was kind of touching on before... that right there is the main thing. The eating stuff is actually the exact thing that scared me into coming in to see you in the first place. Because my appetite has just been completely all over the place, and I mean, it makes zero sense to me anymore. It’s like my body doesn't know what it wants. \n\nSome days, I will literally just forget meals completely. I’ll be driving around to the warehouses, dealing with the suppliers, and I’ll get to, like, seven o'clock at night and realize I haven't eaten a single solid thing all day. Not even a passing thought about food. My stomach doesn't even growl, it's like the hunger signal is just broken. But then... then you have these other nights where it’s the total extreme opposite. I'll be up past midnight, and suddenly it's like this switch flips and I am just absolutely ravenous. I end up tearing through whatever takeout I can get delivered that late, plus whatever random snacks are sitting around in my cupboards. I'm talking, like, half a pizza, a family-sized bag of chips, cookies, just tearing through wrappers and eating whatever I can get my hands on until I feel completely gross. \n\nAnd because of this whole chaotic cycle—the fasting all day and then the massive midnight binges—my weight is just... I don't even know, to be honest. I refuse to step on a scale right now. Some days my suit pants feel loose because I haven't eaten in two days, and other days the waistband is digging into me from all the late-night junk food and bloating. It's just a constant back-and-forth, and realizing how out of control that specific part of my routine has gotten... yeah, that's really what terrified me enough to finally pick up the phone and make this appointment. I just can't keep living like a pendulum swinging between starving and stuffed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or thinking clearly lately, like it's harder to focus or make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying earlier about work, it’s... it’s really bad. The concentration thing is huge right now. Because, you know, in sales, you have to be quick. You have to be able to read a room, remember a client's last order, know the profit margins off the top of your head. But lately? It’s like my brain is just wading through wet cement. \n\nI’ll be sitting at my desk, right? And I’ll have my CRM software open—that’s the program we use to track all the client interactions and orders and stuff—and I’ll just stare at it. I'll click on a client's profile, maybe it's this big account I have out in Ohio, and I know I need to make a call to negotiate a new bulk rate for these industrial fasteners we sell. Normally, I'd look at their history, instantly know we can offer a five percent discount, pick up the phone, and close it in ten minutes. But now... I just look at the screen. The cursor is just blinking and blinking, and I can't even process what I'm looking at. I'll read the same email from my supplier like four or five times, and by the end of it, I still have absolutely no idea what they just said. It’s like the words are just floating there, completely disconnected from any meaning. \n\nAnd making decisions? Forget about it. It’s not just the big work decisions, either. It’s the stupid, everyday stuff. Like, I’ll get in my car in the morning to start my route, and I’ll just sit in my driveway with the engine running for, I don't know, twenty minutes, just agonizing over whether to take the interstate or the local roads. I'll think about the construction on I-95, and then I'll think about the school zones on the back roads, and I just... I can't pick. I literally cannot make my brain choose a path. It’s paralyzing. \n\nThat’s exactly how I ended up transposing those numbers on that massive order I told you about earlier. I was looking right at the invoice, I knew what the quantity was supposed to be, but my focus was just so completely shot that my hands typed something entirely different and my brain didn't even catch it. It's exhausting, honestly. You spend your whole day just trying to force your brain to work the way it used to, and it just refuses to cooperate.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah... yeah, honestly, that’s been eating me alive. Just constantly. Because I keep... I mean, I keep replaying everything in my head, especially with work. There are these accounts up in Providence, right? And I had been working them for months. I mean, driving up there, taking the purchasing managers out, doing all the groundwork to get them to switch over to our supply lines. It was supposed to be this huge win. But lately, I just... I keep replaying every single mistake I’ve made with those Providence accounts. Every missed email, every time I hesitated on a call, every tiny little screw-up. I'll just be lying in bed, staring at the ceiling, and my brain just forces me to watch a highlight reel of every time I dropped the ball with them. 

And it’s not just like, 'Oh, I made a mistake, I'll do better tomorrow.' It’s this overwhelming, suffocating feeling that I completely blew my whole future. Like, I’ve ruined my career and there's no coming back from it. I sit at my desk now and I just feel so incredibly useless. I look at the phone and I think, what’s the point in even picking it up? I’m just going to mess it up anyway. I used to be a top earner, and now I feel like I'm just taking up space in that office. 

And the worst part—the absolute worst part—is that I feel like everybody at work can see it. I walk past the conference room or the break area, and I just know they’re looking at me. I can feel them thinking about how far I've fallen off, wondering why the boss hasn't fired me yet. Even the new guys, the ones I was telling you about earlier, I feel like they look at me and just see a total fraud. I feel like they all know I'm useless now. 

And I just blame myself for all of it. Even things that maybe... maybe a few months ago I would have realized were just normal supply chain hiccups, you know? Like a delayed shipment or a manufacturer backorder, stuff that's technically out of my control. Now, I just take it all on myself. I think, 'If I was just better at my job, if I had anticipated this, if I wasn't such a failure, this wouldn't have happened.' It’s just this constant, heavy guilt that I can't shake off, no matter what I do. I just feel like I'm dragging the whole team down and everyone is just waiting for me to finally pack up my desk.

Between the job going sideways and my apartment just being littered with late-night pizza boxes and feeling like a mess... work and home both just feel like they’re completely slipping out of my control right now. And I hate that feeling. I've always been the guy who has it together, so feeling like I'm losing my grip on everything is... yeah, it's really distressing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you experienced days where you feel down or depressed most of the day, or have lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Down or depressed... yeah, I guess that’s a good way to put it. But it’s almost more like... it’s like everything has just gone gray. The things I used to actually look forward to just feel like chores now, or I just avoid them entirely. \n\nLike, take my weekends, for example. I’ve been in this Sunday league for soccer for, man, probably three years now. We’re not professionals or anything, obviously, but it was a big deal to me. I was always the guy organizing the post-game drinks, making sure everyone showed up, getting the team hyped. But the last couple of weeks? I haven’t even replied to the group chat. I just... I look at my phone, I see the messages rolling in about who's bringing the net or whatever, and I can’t even muster the energy to type \"I’m out.\" I just leave them on read. I don't care at all. \n\nAnd it’s not just soccer, it’s literally everything. My girlfriend, she’s been trying to be patient with me, but I used to really love cooking for her. I’d actually go out of my way to hit the good grocery store, pick out fresh ingredients, do this whole elaborate pasta from scratch or a really nice steak dinner. It was our thing, you know? A way to unwind and connect after a crazy week of driving and sales calls. Now, the thought of standing in the kitchen and chopping an onion feels like I'm being asked to climb Mount Everest. We’ve just been getting takeout, or like I said, I don't eat at all until those 2 AM binges. I just can’t make myself care about making a nice meal anymore. \n\nWe used to do this other thing, too, where we’d go check out these small art galleries downtown on Saturday afternoons. I’m not some huge art expert or anything, but I always liked the vibe, seeing what local people were creating, just walking around and talking about it. Now, she asks if I want to go and I just stare at the wall. The whole idea just seems so incredibly pointless to me right now. \n\nEven at work—and I know I told you work is going terribly right now—but even the parts I used to actually like are just dead to me. I always took pride in helping the newer sales guys. You know, the young guys fresh out of school who don’t really know how to talk to a tough manufacturing client yet. I used to sit with them, review their pitches, give them pointers on how to close. It felt good to be the guy they looked up to. But last week, this new guy came up to my desk asking for advice on a tricky account, and I just completely brushed him off. I didn’t even look at his file. I just told him he needed to figure it out himself and put my headphones on. I just... I can't make myself care about any of it. Not the soccer, not the cooking, the galleries, the mentoring... it’s like someone just unplugged me from everything that used to make me feel like myself. So yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\nPatient: Honestly, like I was kind of touching on before... that right there is the main thing. The eating stuff is actually the exact thing that scared me into coming in to see you in the first place. Because my appetite has just been completely all over the place, and I mean, it makes zero sense to me anymore. It’s like my body doesn't know what it wants. \n\nSome days, I will literally just forget meals completely. I’ll be driving around to the warehouses, dealing with the suppliers, and I’ll get to, like, seven o'clock at night and realize I haven't eaten a single solid thing all day. Not even a passing thought about food. My stomach doesn't even growl, it's like the hunger signal is just broken. But then... then you have these other nights where it’s the total extreme opposite. I'll be up past midnight, and suddenly it's like this switch flips and I am just absolutely ravenous. I end up tearing through whatever takeout I can get delivered that late, plus whatever random snacks are sitting around in my cupboards. I'm talking, like, half a pizza, a family-sized bag of chips, cookies, just tearing through wrappers and eating whatever I can get my hands on until I feel completely gross. \n\nAnd because of this whole chaotic cycle—the fasting all day and then the massive midnight binges—my weight is just... I don't even know, to be honest. I refuse to step on a scale right now. Some days my suit pants feel loose because I haven't eaten in two days, and other days the waistband is digging into me from all the late-night junk food and bloating. It's just a constant back-and-forth, and realizing how out of control that specific part of my routine has gotten... yeah, that's really what terrified me enough to finally pick up the phone and make this appointment. I just can't keep living like a pendulum swinging between starving and stuffed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or thinking clearly lately, like it's harder to focus or make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying earlier about work, it’s... it’s really bad. The concentration thing is huge right now. Because, you know, in sales, you have to be quick. You have to be able to read a room, remember a client's last order, know the profit margins off the top of your head. But lately? It’s like my brain is just wading through wet cement. \n\nI’ll be sitting at my desk, right? And I’ll have my CRM software open—that’s the program we use to track all the client interactions and orders and stuff—and I’ll just stare at it. I'll click on a client's profile, maybe it's this big account I have out in Ohio, and I know I need to make a call to negotiate a new bulk rate for these industrial fasteners we sell. Normally, I'd look at their history, instantly know we can offer a five percent discount, pick up the phone, and close it in ten minutes. But now... I just look at the screen. The cursor is just blinking and blinking, and I can't even process what I'm looking at. I'll read the same email from my supplier like four or five times, and by the end of it, I still have absolutely no idea what they just said. It’s like the words are just floating there, completely disconnected from any meaning. \n\nAnd making decisions? Forget about it. It’s not just the big work decisions, either. It’s the stupid, everyday stuff. Like, I’ll get in my car in the morning to start my route, and I’ll just sit in my driveway with the engine running for, I don't know, twenty minutes, just agonizing over whether to take the interstate or the local roads. I'll think about the construction on I-95, and then I'll think about the school zones on the back roads, and I just... I can't pick. I literally cannot make my brain choose a path. It’s paralyzing. \n\nThat’s exactly how I ended up transposing those numbers on that massive order I told you about earlier. I was looking right at the invoice, I knew what the quantity was supposed to be, but my focus was just so completely shot that my hands typed something entirely different and my brain didn't even catch it. It's exhausting, honestly. You spend your whole day just trying to force your brain to work the way it used to, and it just refuses to cooperate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough at things lately, or found yourself blaming yourself for things that aren't really your fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, honestly, that’s been eating me alive. I keep replaying every single mistake I’ve made with those Providence accounts. Every missed email, every time I hesitated on a call, every tiny little screw-up. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, the energy thing... it’s just gone. Completely zapped. I mean, you’d think with me not doing much besides sitting in my car or at my desk, I’d have plenty of energy, but it’s the exact opposite. 

I try to rest, I really do. Sometimes on a Sunday, I will literally just stay on the couch all day. I won't go out, I won't do chores, I'll just lie down for hours and hours, not doing a single physical thing. You’d think after doing absolutely nothing for a whole weekend, I’d wake up on Monday morning refreshed and ready to hit the road, right? But no. Even when I do lie down for hours like that, my body just feels completely drained the next day. It’s this deep, heavy kind of exhaustion, like my limbs are made of concrete or like I ran a marathon in my sleep. 

And it’s really starting to become a huge problem for my route, because, you know, my job isn't manual labor, but there is a physical element to it. I have these sample cases I have to lug around to the different warehouses and client offices. They’re these big, heavy-duty hard cases filled with all our physical product—industrial fasteners, heavy-gauge brackets, metal samples, all that stuff. Usually, I don't even think about it. I just pop the trunk, grab a case in each hand, and stride right into the building. 

But lately? Oh man. Just carrying those sample cases into a client meeting takes way, way more out of me than it should. I’ll pull into the visitor parking lot, look at my trunk, and just dread having to lift them. By the time I get them out of the car and walk across the parking lot to the lobby, I am practically winded. My arms feel like lead, and my legs are just dragging. I have to stand there in the elevator and take these deep breaths, just trying to compose myself so I don't walk into the purchasing manager's office looking like I'm about to collapse. 

It’s just embarrassing, honestly. In sales, you have to be the high-energy guy. You have to bring the enthusiasm into the room. But how are you supposed to do that when just carrying your own materials into the building makes you want to lie down on the floor and go to sleep? I just don't understand where all my stamina went. It's like my battery is permanently stuck at one percent, no matter how long I leave it on the charger. I'm just so tired all the time, even when I haven't done anything to earn it.

But... honestly, the thing that actually scared me into coming in today is that my eating has just been completely all over the place lately. \n\nAnd I don't just mean skipping a lunch because I got busy with a client or something. I mean, some days I will literally forget meals completely. Like, the whole day will go by, the sun goes down, and I'll suddenly realize I haven't had a single bite of food, not even a protein bar from the console of my car. There are these other nights where I am just tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight. It's like a switch flips and I can't stop. I'll order a ridiculous amount of food on my phone, or I'll just be standing in my kitchen at 2 AM ripping open bags of chips or whatever I can find, eating like I haven't seen food in a month. It's just so extreme, going from absolutely nothing to just gorging myself in the middle of the night, and it really started freaking me out because I've never had this happen before. Usually I'm just a normal three-meals-a-day kind of guy, you know? So seeing myself act like this... yeah, it scared me into making the appointment.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating a lot of food in a short period of time, like in the middle of the night, do you feel like you can’t stop or control how much you eat during those moments?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control, really. It's not like I'm sitting there thinking, \"Oh God, I have to stop, I have to stop,\" and my hands are just moving on their own, you know? It's more like... I'm just incredibly hungry all of a sudden. Like, ravenous. And because I probably haven't eaten all day—like I was saying about just totally forgetting to eat when I'm on the road or making calls—by the time 2 AM rolls around, my body is just screaming for calories. \n\nSo when I'm standing there in the kitchen, ripping into a bag of chips or waiting for the delivery guy, I'm fully aware of what I'm doing. I want to eat all of it. I'm choosing to eat it because it tastes so good in that moment and I feel like I need it to make up for the whole day. It's just the amount that's weird for me. I'll order, like, two large pizzas and some wings, and I'll actually eat most of it, which is insane for one guy. But I never feel like some outside force is making me do it or that I couldn't put the slice down if I really wanted to. I just... don't want to put it down. I just want to keep eating until I'm full, which just takes a lot more food than it ever used to. It's more of an intense urge that I'm willingly giving in to, rather than losing control, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that these episodes of eating a lot happen regularly, maybe once a week or more, and do you ever try to make up for them by doing something like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing a lot of extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, the frequency is kind of hard to pin down exactly, because my schedule is so erratic with the driving and the client dinners and all the prep work I do in the evenings. But if I had to put a number on it... yeah, I guess it probably happens at least once a week, maybe twice if it's a particularly intense week where I've been running on fumes and then suddenly crash. It's not like a scheduled thing, obviously, just whenever that switch flips and I realize I'm starving at two in the morning. \n\nBut as for the other stuff you mentioned—making myself sick or using laxatives or whatever—no, absolutely not. I've never done anything like that. The thought of throwing up actually really grosses me out, to be honest. I mean, I hate being sick. And I definitely don't take laxatives. I had this whole routine, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, just doing some weights and a little cardio to stay in shape for the job, because you know, in sales, appearances matter, you want to look sharp in a suit. But lately? I haven't been to the gym in... I don't even know, weeks? Months? I just haven't had the time or the focus for it. So I'm definitely not running five miles the next day to burn off the pizza. I usually just wake up feeling kind of sluggish and gross, maybe a little bloated, and then I just grab a coffee, hit the road, and the whole cycle starts over again where I'm too busy to eat all day. So no, I don't do anything to try and undo it, I just sort of live with the aftermath until the next time it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot or skipping meals cause you a lot of distress, or do they interfere with your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, distress is... well, yes.
